# ATM Bug and Optimization Backlog

This backlog captures ordinary ATM product/framework bugs, dogfood failures, workflow friction, and optimization ideas. It is not the release incident deny-list. Use `docs/INCIDENT_RESPONSE.md` and `known-bad-versions.json` only for published releases that can cause data loss, corrupt governance state, violate licensing constraints, or ship critical release-trust defects.

AI routing note: use `.agents/skills/atm-bug-backlog/SKILL.md` before writing backlog entries. The skill must first classify whether the issue belongs to ATM or to another project repo.

2026-07-11 follow-up:

- `ATM-BUG-2026-07-07-048` is now fixed by running cached onefile dist in-process instead of spawning a second Node process for every invocation. `npm run validate:next-warm-run-latency -- --json` passed on Windows with `cliLogicMs: 39`, warm-run `p50Ms: 1693.09`, `p95Ms: 5195.47`, and `wrapperOverheadMs: 1998.98`, all within the declared budgets. `npm run typecheck`, runner entrypoint regression, and frozen release build also passed.
- `ATM-BUG-2026-07-11-091` records the route friction found while starting this closeout: backlog IDs were treated as missing ledger task IDs by prompt-scoped `next`, then misrouted to `create-atom` by `start`.

Do not record non-ATM project bugs here. For adopter/app/game/site/data bugs, create or update `docs/governance/project-bug-and-optimization-backlog.md` inside the affected repo.

2026-07-09 sync note:

- `ATM-BUG-2026-07-09-059` is fixed by adding a read-only `ATM_NEXT_TASK_SCOPE_AUDIT_ADVISORY` route for missing task-like scope when `requestedAction` is `audit` or `analyze`; mutation-oriented missing scope remains fail-closed.
- `ATM-BUG-2026-07-07-047` was already fixed before this sync; `npm run validate:prompt-scoped-next` now covers broad all/open/remaining task-card prompts and confirms they route to the discovered batch queue instead of `ATM_NEXT_TASK_SCOPE_NOT_FOUND`.
- `ATM-BUG-2026-07-08-056` remains observable: after releasing and re-claiming a clean `ATM-FRAMEWORK-TEMP-codex-captain` lock, `doctor` / `tasks audit` can still report it as `stale-completed` with an old linked task even though the lock JSON no longer contains `linkedTaskId`. This is advisory and did not block `hook pre-push`, but it should be fixed before relying on stale-lock audit as a precise signal.
- `ATM-BUG-2026-07-08-056` is fixed by moving framework lock auto-reconcile audit records to `.atm/runtime/framework-lock-auto-reconcile.jsonl` and by preventing active unlabeled temp locks from inferring stale linked-task context from older actor sessions. Regression coverage lives in `validate:framework-development-governance` and `validate:task-ledger-governance`.
- `ATM-BUG-2026-07-07-045` / `ATM-BUG-2026-07-07-053` are fixed by expanding `git commit` attempt status into a recoverable incident packet: every attempt now records HEAD before/after, whether HEAD advanced, `commit-status` and retry commands, raw copyable git command, and wrapper-staged residue rollback. The deadlock reason was that outer caller timeout or empty stdout/stderr left the operator unable to tell whether the commit had already landed, so retrying risked either duplicate work or manual index archaeology.
- `ATM-BUG-2026-07-09-060` is fixed by raising the governed `git commit` wrapper default timeout from 120s to 420s. The deadlock reason was that a normal framework pre-commit path took about 277s and passed, but the wrapper killed host git at 120s, reporting `ATM_GIT_COMMIT_TIMEOUT` even though the hook was not failing.
- `ATM-BUG-2026-07-07-043` is fixed at the commit-bundle preflight layer: a task with an active `closeout-only` claim now fails before auto-staging or pre-commit hooks when the bundle contains in-scope source mutations, returning `ATM_GIT_COMMIT_CLOSEOUT_ONLY_MUTATION` and the exact `next --claim --claim-intent write` recovery command.
- `ATM-BUG-2026-07-07-046`, `ATM-BUG-2026-07-07-049`, and `ATM-BUG-2026-07-07-050` were already fixed in the taskflow close atomicity lane: taskflow close commits now resolve actor-scoped git identity, commit from a temporary index, reset task-owned live-index residue after successful close commits, and share dry-run/write closeback-path readiness. The stale backlog rows were causing governance rework loops, so this sync marks the source-backed fixes as closed.
- `ATM-BUG-2026-07-08-056` is fixed by keeping framework lock auto-reconcile audit in `.atm/runtime/framework-lock-auto-reconcile.jsonl` instead of the protected history evidence singleton. This prevents `git commit --auto-stage` from picking up an uncommittable protected evidence file after same-actor stale temp-lock cleanup.
- `ATM-BUG-2026-07-07-047`, `ATM-BUG-2026-07-07-052`, and `ATM-BUG-2026-07-07-054` are fixed and verified: blanket all/open task-card prompts route to batch instead of `ATM_NEXT_TASK_SCOPE_NOT_FOUND`; protected override audit events survive/stage correctly; resumable validator runs expose status/resume behavior. The table rows were stale, so this sync removes three false High-priority governance blockers from the queue.
- `ATM-BUG-2026-06-15-008` is fixed by keeping cross-repo planning absolute paths out of Team Agents `file.write` leases: target repo write paths still require explicit target scope, while planning-repo absolute paths can route as read-only planning context without `ATM_TEAM_WRITE_SCOPE_TRAVERSAL`. Regression coverage lives in `node --strip-types scripts/validate-team-agents.ts --case permission-lease`.
- `ATM-BUG-2026-06-15-010` was already fixed by the historical-batch evidence lane: `evidence historical-batch` writes shared envelopes and task slices, and `taskflow close --historical-batch` consumes them with commit-range and close-readiness safeguards. The row was stale and is now synced to source reality.
- Dogfood note: this repair pass also showed a small workflow friction where the agent began a two-file quickfix after code localization before acquiring the framework temp claim. The claim was acquired immediately afterward and covered the touched files, but the root reason is that the fast playbook's "claim before edit" instruction is easy to miss once a bug is localized inside a large file. Keep improving `next`/skill UX so claim scope can be suggested from discovered candidate files before the first patch.
- Dogfood note: `ATM-BUG-2026-07-09-061` is fixed by adding `check:encoding:touched` and `check:encoding:staged` npm scripts backed by ATM's canonical `guard encoding` command. The touched wrapper includes untracked text files so newly created scripts/docs are checked before first staging.
- Dogfood note: `ATM-BUG-2026-07-09-062` is fixed by aligning the framework-maintenance fast playbook with the route command. The deadlock reason was that `next --prompt` correctly returned `framework-temp-claim-required` and a `framework-mode claim` command, but the embedded fast playbook still told agents to run `next --claim`, which fails with `ATM_NEXT_CLAIM_NO_TASK` when no imported task queue exists.
- Dogfood note: `ATM-BUG-2026-07-09-063` is fixed in this pass: `taskflow open --write` correctly fell back without a profile, but the recommended `tasks new --template aao-l2-split` path then failed because the framework runner had no bundled markdown template assets. The deadlock reason was that the documented low-level generator surface advertised a default template that could not be loaded in either frozen or source-first mode. The fix adds the default template asset to the markdown task-source package and locks source/frozen generation coverage in `tests/cli/tasks-new.test.ts`.
- Dogfood note: `ATM-BUG-2026-07-09-064` is fixed in this pass: `node atm.mjs git commit` can exceed a normal agent command timeout because its pre-commit hook runs heavyweight validators such as `validate:git-head-evidence`. The commit may still complete after the caller times out, so `git commit-status` now reports live branch commit queue diagnostics, including lock path, owner PID liveness, age, current HEAD, and wait-vs-inspect guidance.
- `ATM-BUG-2026-07-07-051` was already fixed in the emergency gate lane: `ATM_EMERGENCY_LANE_APPROVAL_REQUIRED` now includes required `--allowed-flag` entries for protected flags such as `--historical-delivery`, and the message warns that omitting them will later fail with `ATM_EMERGENCY_FLAG_NOT_APPROVED`. The stale row was keeping a resolved emergency-lane UX issue in the active queue.
- `ATM-BUG-2026-07-07-044` was already fixed in the prompt-scoped next lane: re-claim with escalated intent preserves scope-amended `claim.files` instead of rebuilding only from static task scope. The stale row was keeping a resolved claim lifecycle bug in the active queue.
- `ATM-BUG-2026-07-07-042` was already fixed in the prompt-scoped next validator: `teamPlanResult` is dumped only when assertions fail, so successful runs stay transcript-friendly. The stale row was keeping a resolved validator-noise issue in the active queue.
- `ATM-BUG-2026-07-09-059` was already fixed in the prompt-scoped next audit advisory lane: retrospective audit prompts that mention non-ledger historical labels now get `ATM_NEXT_TASK_SCOPE_AUDIT_ADVISORY` instead of the hard `ATM_NEXT_TASK_SCOPE_NOT_FOUND` blocker.
- `ATM-BUG-2026-06-15-004` is verified fixed in this pass: `taskflow close --historical-delivery` now supports the explicit operator waiver lane through `--waiver-out-of-scope-delivery --reason <text>` and the `--waive-out-of-scope` alias, while historical-delivery provenance records waived out-of-scope files and the waiver reason. The stale row kept a resolved closeback UX issue open; the proof path was briefly blocked by stale `taskflow-dryrun` assertions that still expected older commit-bundle/evidence behavior.
- `ATM-BUG-2026-07-07-055` is fixed by extending the taskflow close commit-bundle regression to cover stale staged bundle manifests: a same-task `.bundle-manifest.json` staged at an older version is reset out of the live index after the temp-index close commit lands the fresh version.
- `ATM-BUG-2026-06-16-014` is fixed by the historical-batch diagnostic disposition lane: partial or diagnostic-only slices can now be explicitly finalized as `keep-diagnostic`, `abandon`, or `remove-evidence`, while close-ready slices are refused and must still go through `taskflow close --historical-batch`.
- `ATM-BUG-2026-07-07-041` is fixed by deepening default `next` output compaction: non-verbose results now keep `evidence.nextAction` as the authoritative route payload, remove duplicated top-level aliases, keep playbook message echoes compact, and make `--verbose` a real parser-supported escape hatch for the full envelope.
- Dogfood note (2026-07-10 RFT batch `0002/0004/0005/0006/0007`): mechanical-split delivery friction, done-task import lockout, closeout exit-code ambiguity, and planning-index lag were recorded as `ATM-BUG-2026-07-10-076` through `ATM-BUG-2026-07-10-080`. Planning-mirror residue after successful closeback remains tracked in `ATM-BUG-2026-07-10-074`, with additional RFT cross-repo `source.planPath` evidence added in that row.

| ATM-BUG-2026-07-12-161 | 2026-07-12 | AI-Atomic-Framework | Bug / Product gap | P0 | Fixed in TASK-GIT-0015 | Team Broker / staging index arbitration | Multiple AI captains can hold parallel-safe Broker lanes, but the shared Git index has no owner/lease model. Fable's untracked `.atm/history/evidence/TASK-AAO-FABLE-003.bundle-manifest.json` blocked `node atm.mjs git commit` with `ATM_HOOK_GENERATED_RESIDUE_BLOCKED`; a subsequent operator path suggested unstaging foreign `taskflow.ts`, exposing that agents can mutate another actor's staged index state outside Broker arbitration. User then clarified that `git restore` and similarly severe Git commands must also be prohibited unless a highest-specific authorization keyword is present. Additional live repro while closing TASK-AAO-0188: `taskflow pre-close` recommended `git restore --staged` against foreign staged `packages/cli/src/commands/hook/pre-commit.ts`; `git commit --defer-foreign-staged --dry-run` accepted foreign FABLE bundle manifests as deferred warnings, but the real pre-commit hook re-blocked the same residue because wrapper defer semantics are not transmitted to the hook; `tasks release` left nested `taskDirectionLock.status: active` in released locks, so Broker still reported active-task-scope conflicts; an emergency gitHookBypass lease was consumed by a failed Broker-conflict preflight before any commit was created; and the recovery hint pointed to `backend.brokerConflictOverride`, which would be too dangerous without a paper resolution artifact because it could bypass protection for another agent's staged file. Follow-up repro while trying to safely finish FABLE-005: `next --claim` ignored a freshly written `atm.brokerConflictResolution.v1` artifact (`BCR-9ybygk`) and still reported missing resolution; `--no-verify --dry-run` only surfaced `ATM_EMERGENCY_LANE_APPROVAL_REQUIRED`, but the real commit then surfaced `ATM_GIT_COMMIT_BROKER_CONFLICT_OVERRIDE_REQUIRED` for TASK-AAO-0188/TASK-GIT-0013, so dry-run did not faithfully predict the write gate and can consume an emergency lease on a commit that never reaches Git. 2026-07-13: `TASK-AAO-0189` was formalized as `TASK-GIT-0015` and closed with delivery `329890f7`, close `f8e7a6f`, and planning mirror `cfb724de`. The implementation adds shared git-index ownership evidence, `ATM_INDEX_FOREIGN_ACTIVE_STAGED`, `indexLane`, ATM stage/destructive override lease creation, removes raw `git restore --staged` and dirty-file `git restore --` remediation from core taskflow/commit paths, and keeps TASK-AAO-0188 done/no-residue with `node --strip-types tests/cli/task-ledger-fixture-isolation.test.ts` passing. | Keep follow-up rows 168-170 for remaining UX/performance refinements; core P0 staging-index arbitration is closed by TASK-GIT-0015. |

The file diff was non-trivial (`144 insertions(+), 14 deletions(-)`), so blindly restoring or stashing would risk losing another lane's implementation progress. A follow-up preservation attempt split the WIP after `ATM_TOUCHED_PHYSICAL_LINE_BUDGET_BLOCKED`; Batch A successfully committed ledger state as `e5883bca65d50c111677c8dd28fb29ffb4242a81`, but Batch B source preservation failed with `ATM_HOOK_PRE_COMMIT_FAILED` because `npm run typecheck` rejected the interrupted WIP. The failure was not a line-budget issue and had no recoveryCommand. Implement one public reconciliation operation that consumes the durable receipt, verifies the recorded HEAD and candidate lineage, waits or queues only for the short index mutation, retries idempotently, preserves concurrent foreign blob ids, and marks the receipt clean only after an independent index oracle passes. Add deterministic tests for lock held before reconciliation, lock released then retry, HEAD moved after receipt, repeated retry, and concurrent foreign index mutation. Treat candidate-scope contamination and live-surface pre-commit/record-commit analysis as related but separately testable seams; do not hard-code task IDs, path lists, entry counts, or sleep durations. 2026-08-13 amplification evidence: consecutive reconciliation failures accumulate. After six governed commits taken while one orphaned .git/index.lock persisted, the live index carried 44 staged-deletion entries for files that HEAD already contained, and pre-commit began demanding broker compose receipts for them. The boundary is now precisely measured: governed commit and governed push both run entirely through the sealed candidate index and completed normally throughout, while taskflow close cannot — it either runs git restore --worktree under --defer-foreign-state, or trips ATM_CLOSE_WINDOW_FOREIGN_STAGED_TASKS on the accumulated residue without it. So a single orphaned lock does not stop delivery, but it does stop closure, and the damage compounds with every commit taken in that state. A retry that only repairs the current commit is therefore not sufficient; the reconciliation debt itself has to be drainable. 2026-08-27 remaining scope after the contention quickfix: the retry closes the common transient case and makes the debt enumerable, but the durable drain is still not a public operation. Attach the successor repair card for (a) an operation that consumes the ATM_LIVE_INDEX_RECONCILIATION_FAILED receipt itself, verifies recorded HEAD and candidate lineage, and drains accumulated debt across several commits rather than only the current one — recoverLiveIndexAfterSuccessfulCommit already recovers a single named commit and is the natural seam to generalise; (b) the taskflow close path, which is the surface that actually breaks under accumulated residue, since governed commit and push both continue to run through the sealed candidate index; and (c) an independent index oracle that marks a receipt clean. The retry policy defaults are deliberately configurable rather than hard-coded so a successor card can tune or replace them without touching the reconciliation loop. 2026-08-28 remaining scope after the drain quickfix: (a) the 88 receipts already on disk predate lineage and are reported honestly as missing-lineage rather than guessed at — they were deliberately not rewritten, and draining them needs either an operator-supplied commit through the existing --commit route or a separate bounded backfill decision; (b) the taskflow close surface is still the place accumulated residue actually breaks delivery, and was deliberately not touched here; (c) an independent index oracle that marks a receipt clean after a drain, and the receipt lifecycle question of who clears a receipt whose debt is fully drained, are both still open. 2026-08-28 after slice A the remaining slices are explicitly sequenced and paused: (B) deferral release must not restore entries that were drained rather than parked, which depends on A; (C) draining automatically inside the close window is blocked pending an owner ruling on cross-card index authority, because reconciliation residue is attributed to the committing task while it blocks a different task's close, so close would have to consume another card's receipt and mutate the shared index on its behalf; (D) the 82 legacy receipts that carry retained paths without lineage remain undrainable and were deliberately not rewritten. One housekeeping item: ATM_CLOSE_WINDOW_UNRECONCILED_RESIDUE is covered by the registry's ATM_ catch-all prefix rule, exactly like its sibling ATM_CLOSE_WINDOW_FOREIGN_STAGED_TASKS which also has no exact entry, so no registry edit was required; docs/ERROR_CODES.md was deliberately NOT regenerated because `npm run generate:error-codes` rewrites source line references repository-wide and would have pulled unrelated lanes' drift into this commit. Regenerating that index is a separate housekeeping pass. 2026-08-29 read-only design review (claude-008), no code written: a measured correction to this item's own (D) strand, the confirmed mechanism behind (B), and one precision gap left by slice A. Measurement, reproducible by parsing every .atm/history/evidence/*.live-index-reconciliation.json: 88 receipts exist; 82 carry retainedPaths and those 82 name exactly ONE distinct path between them, .atm/history/evidence/git-head.jsonl, every entry with reason worktree-diverged and failure null; the 6 receipts that actually carry ATM_LIVE_INDEX_RECONCILIATION_FAILED (ATM-GOV-0315, 0331, 0352, 0353, 0355, 0385) carry ZERO retainedPaths; and the live index of this working tree currently holds 0 staged entries. Three consequences follow. (1) The "82 legacy receipts without lineage" this item described as undrainable debt are not reconciliation debt at all: git-head.jsonl is an append-only governance ledger that the commit itself appends to, so its worktree necessarily diverges from HEAD at reconciliation time and the path is retained benignly and correctly. No drain can or should advance it, and no lineage backfill would change that. (2) The 6 genuinely failed receipts name no path at all, so they are structurally undrainable by any receipt-consuming operation; whatever debt they represent was never recorded per-path. Draining legacy receipts is therefore not the open work (D) implied. (3) Because the live index is empty, the slice A residue classifier cannot fire in this repository today and neither B nor C can be exercised; they are forward-looking insurance rather than current breakage, and sequencing should reflect that. Mechanism behind B, confirmed by reading close-window-lock.ts: the close window passes the FULL unexpectedStagedFiles set, not a foreign subset, into deferForeignStagedFiles, which runs git restore --staged over it. For a residue path that call IS the drain, because it sets the index entry to HEAD. Release then restores every snapshot entry byte-for-byte unconditionally, which precisely reconstructs the debt the defer had accidentally repaired. B is therefore not a tweak to make restore skip a drained entry: at that point the code cannot even distinguish its own receipt's residue from another card's residue. Recommended split. B1 is implement-now and needs no policy: record a per-path receiptTaskId alongside each deferred entry and disclose it on release, changing no restore semantics. B1 is the mechanical gate that stops a later B2 from silently bypassing C's cross-card authority ruling, because today the data needed to make that distinction does not exist in the lock record. B2, actually skipping restoration for a proven-drained entry, should be folded into C rather than shipped alone: if C drains before the snapshot is taken, residue never enters the snapshot and B2 is eliminated by construction. C remains needs-owner-policy. Its engineering proof is already complete, since a path advances only when the current index entry equals the parent tree of its firstUnreconciledCommit AND the worktree equals HEAD; the missing piece is authority alone. The recommended shape follows the --defer-foreign-staged precedent: an explicit operator-carried opt-in such as --drain-residue, close still fail-closed by default, ordered lease publication first, then drain, then snapshot of the foreign remainder. D should be re-scoped rather than backfilled, into two disjoint populations that no longer share a remedy: P1, the 82 benign ledger retentions of .atm/history/evidence/git-head.jsonl, which are correct retentions and need only this record correction rather than any drain or backfill; and P2, the 6 historical ATM_LIVE_INDEX_RECONCILIATION_FAILED receipts (ATM-GOV-0315, 0331, 0352, 0353, 0355, 0385) that carry no retained paths at all and are therefore structurally undrainable by any receipt-consuming operation. Reconstructing lineage from reflog or history is explicitly rejected. Slice A precision gap, unfixed and reported here: diagnoseUnexpectedStaged takes classification.residueSources[0] as THE owner, so when residue spans receipts from several tasks the emitted requiredCommand names an arbitrary one; an operator following it drains part of the debt, close still blocks, and the message does not change. The fix is to emit one drain command per distinct receiptTaskId. Coupling to release work: A, B1 and C are all packages/cli/src source-only and can be written while npm packaging work is in flight, but none of them take effect until the frozen runner is rebuilt, so runner-sync must wait for that release to succeed; since B and C cannot fire today, the cost of waiting is zero. The smallest next change is this record correction itself, which is zero code, followed by B1. |

The only documented escape is a pathspec-only quarantine commit, which needs owner approval, or an emergency lease. | Repair work driven by diagnosis inherently produces changes before the owning card can be written, because what the card must scope is only known after the cause is found. That is the normal shape of fixing a red validator, not an anomaly. The claim path needs an explicit, auditable adoption route — the mirror of --wip-commit — that binds pre-existing unattributed changes to the claim being taken, records what was adopted, and stays fail-closed for anything outside the declared scope. Without it the governed sequence is only usable when the fix is known in advance. | At HEAD bc9cbc453e, eight repaired files (integration-hooks/implementation.ts, tasks/repairclose-orchestrator.ts, __tests__/framework-mode-staged-residue.spec.ts, validate-broker-registry.ts, validate-framework-development-governance.ts, validate-framework-governance-repairs.ts, validate-task-direction-governance/adopter-core.ts, validators/task-ledger/suite-impl/implementation.ts) were left unattributed after ATM-GOV-0356 was authored to own them. next --claim reports ATM_CLAIM_FOREIGN_UNSTAGED_WIP listing those exact files with ownership 'unowned'; tasks claim reports ATM_TASK_CLAIM_NOT_READY for a planned card; next --claim --files naming the same set is refused identically. | Add the claim-side adoption route and a focused fixture proving that adoption is bounded by the declared scope and that anything outside it still fails closed. Until then, the repairs listed above stay in the working tree with ATM-GOV-0356 imported and unclaimed; they are individually verified (typecheck green, and validate-framework-development-governance, validate-broker-registry, validate-framework-hook-scope-filtering, validate-branch-commit-queue all green) and need only a claim to land. Do not resolve this by folding them into an unrelated claimed card; the scope conflation would be worse than the delay. |
| ATM-BUG-2026-08-13-007 | 2026-08-13 | AI-Atomic-Framework | Bug | High | Resolved | A scope amendment never reaches the governed delivery bundle | tasks scope add extends the active direction lock's allowedFiles, but git commit --auto-stage resolves the delivery bundle from the ledger record's scopePaths. The two are never reconciled, so an amended path is editable, passes its validators, and is then silently excluded from the commit with no diagnostic. The only route to land it is tasks import --force, which is a protected emergency surface requiring a lease. | A path the governance layer just authorised for this task must be deliverable by that task. Either the bundle reads the direction lock, or scope add writes both; which one is an implementation choice, but the two views of the task's scope must agree. Silent exclusion is the worst available outcome because the amendment reported success. | Cleanest reproduction, on ATM-GOV-0364 at HEAD f9da40a4a: tasks scope add --write returned ATM_SCOPE_AMENDMENT_APPLIED for docs/governance/atm-bug-and-optimization-backlog.items/ATM-BUG-2026-08-12-001.json, and the immediately following git commit --auto-stage --dry-run resolved a sealed bundle of four entries, all of them governance records (the task ledger, the scope-amendment event, git-head.jsonl and the live-index reconciliation). The amended path does not appear at all. Earlier, on ATM-GOV-0360 at HEAD f8f7789a3, two paths (governance-optimization/plan-3x-4x-objective-audit-2026-07-31.json and docs/reports/plan-3x-4x-closeout-blocker-map.json) were added with tasks scope add --write, which returned ATM_SCOPE_AMENDMENT_APPLIED. Both were edited, both passed their validators, and git commit --auto-stage produced a commit containing the six original scopePaths and neither amended path. The ledger scopePaths array still had length 6 after both amendments. | Resolved by ATM-GOV-0364 in commit b5bf02d16e2e837b3533596b31d9a284133dbc57. syncScopeAmendmentState now also unions the amended paths into the ledger's scopePaths, so the direction lock and the delivery bundle describe the same scope. Union, never replace: an amendment adds reach and does not redefine what the card declared. Verified live after republishing the frozen runner from 88230861e — the same scope add that previously left scopePaths untouched now carries three new paths into it. Regression guard: test_atm_gov_0364_scope_amendment_reaches_the_delivery_bundle in tests/cli/governance-round-trip-cost.test.ts, which covers the union, idempotence, Windows separators, and a task with no declared scope. |
| ATM-BUG-2026-08-13-008 | 2026-08-13 | AI-Atomic-Framework | Bug | High | Resolved | tasks import --force erases the active claim without being asked to | tasks import --write --force resets the ledger record to status planned and drops the active claim even when --force-overwrite-claims is not passed. The transition log for the card records the full sequence: import, import, reserve, promote, repair-claim, claim. | Resolved by ATM-GOV-0364 in commit b5bf02d16e2e837b3533596b31d9a284133dbc57. The preservation branch existed but was gated on parseClaimRecord returning a fully-formed record, so a claim that was live but missing an optional field read as no claim at all. The distinct claim-identity aggregate defect remains open in [ATM-BUG-2026-08-13-017]. Related to [ATM-BUG-2026-08-13-007], which is what forces the import --force in the first place. |
| ATM-BUG-2026-08-13-009 | 2026-08-13 | AI-Atomic-Framework | Bug | Low | Resolved | Withdrawn: pre-close does disclose every prerequisite at once | This item claimed that taskflow pre-close reveals close prerequisites one class at a time, costing a round trip per class. That claim is false and the item is withdrawn. The first pre-close run on ATM-GOV-0360 returned four blockers, and the fourth was staleEvidence carrying the exact list ['validate:cli','validate:git-head-evidence']. Every prerequisite was disclosed on the first call. The appearance of batched disclosure was created entirely by the consumer: the command output was piped through a reader that truncated at 3000 characters, so the last two blockers were never read. | Behaviour as built is already correct. buildHistoricalClosePreflight computes computeMissingValidatorReport unconditionally on every run and appends the stale-evidence blocker alongside the operational ones, so the blocker list is complete regardless of which other classes are currently present. | Re-read of the first pre-close response captured at HEAD f8f7789a3 shows blockers = [unexpectedStagedTasks ATM_INDEX_FOREIGN_ACTIVE_STAGED, scopeTrackedDirtyFiles ATM_TASKFLOW_PRECLOSE_SCOPE_TRACKED_DIRTY, unexpectedStagedNonBundleFiles ATM_TASKFLOW_PRECLOSE_UNEXPECTED_STAGED_FILES, staleEvidence ATM_TASKFLOW_PRECLOSE_STALE_EVIDENCE] with staleEvidence = ['validate:cli','validate:git-head-evidence']. The same two entries appear identically in the second and third runs. Source confirms the design at packages/cli/src/commands/taskflow/historical-close-preflight.ts, where the stale blocker is built from missingReport on every invocation. | No product change. The real defect was in how the diagnostic was consumed, and it is worth stating because it is easy to repeat: truncating a governance response and then reasoning about what is missing produces a confident, wrong conclusion. Read the blocker list in full, or count it, before concluding anything about disclosure. Withdrawn from ATM-GOV-0364, whose ACC-3 was removed for the same reason. |

| ATM-BUG-2026-08-13-011 | 2026-08-13 | AI-Atomic-Framework | Optimization | Low | Resolved | Subcommand help returns the whole command namespace | atm <command> <subcommand> --help returns every option registered for the command, not the options the subcommand accepts. tasks import --help lists roughly ninety flags spanning claim, close, audit, scope, queue, roster and mirror, so the caller cannot tell which of them the subcommand will accept and must discover the right one by failing. | Help for a subcommand lists the flags that subcommand accepts. A flag belonging only to a sibling subcommand does not appear. This is the difference between help being a lookup and help being a haystack, and it is paid on every unfamiliar surface. | tasks import --task and tasks import --card each returned ATM_CLI_USAGE 'tasks import does not support option'. The correct flag is --from. Reaching it required tasks import --help, whose options array contains flags such as --ttl-seconds, --historical-batch and --sync-status that tasks import does not accept. The same shape reoccurs on evidence run, where the required --command is not distinguishable from the rest of the evidence namespace. | Resolved by ATM-GOV-0364 in commits f7a518c4469d291248b4c71f296a7c965bfbf4b0 and 91f599e471a1b48bbbf3f73e94e1ef13d013ae9d. Subcommand ownership was not recorded anywhere, so recording it was the fix: CommandOption gained an optional subcommands list, makeHelpResult narrows to the subcommand parsed from argv, and ten tasks options declare ownership as the first adopters — tasks import --help drops from 67 flags to 61 with --map, --wip-commit, --discard-wip, --reserved-ok, --all-stale and --apply gone. A flag that has not declared ownership is still listed rather than hidden, and the response reports declared versus undeclared counts so an unfiltered list reads as an incomplete spec rather than as an answer. Remaining work is data, not design: the other tasks options, and the other command specs, can declare ownership incrementally without further code change. Regression guard: test_atm_gov_0364_help_is_scoped_to_the_subcommand in tests/cli/governance-round-trip-cost.test.ts, which asserts against the real tasks spec that import is strictly narrower and that six named sibling-only flags are absent. |
| ATM-BUG-2026-08-13-012 | 2026-08-13 | AI-Atomic-Framework | Bug | High | Closed — staged-deletion tombstones are representable in stage-override leases; re-verified 2026-09-02 | Stage-override lease cannot cover a staged deletion, so close has no route past one | taskflow pre-close refuses to close while foreign staged entries sit in the live index, and names git lease stage-override as the sanctioned route past them. | ATM-BUG-2026-08-13-021 | 2026-08-13 | AI-Atomic-Framework | Bug | Medium | Resolved | Lane refusal does not disclose the owner lane it is comparing against | ATM_LANE_BORROWED_ACTOR_BLOCKED refuses a governed mutation when the executing lane is not the owner lane, but the message names neither the owner lane it expected nor where that value is recorded. The owner lane is already in the caller's hands: the ledger claim carries claim.laneSession.laneSessionId together with an exportHint that is the exact command needed to proceed. An actor that has minted more than one lane session in a session therefore has no way to tell which of them is authoritative except by guessing, and guessing across lanes is exactly the shape of probing a governed gate that the framework otherwise forbids. | A refusal must disclose the record that decided it. The lane refusal should report the ledger path it read the owner claim from, whether that claim records a lane and an export hint, and a command that reads it. It must not disclose the lane value itself under any condition — --actor is attribution, not authority, so a rule of the form 'reveal the hint when the caller presents the owner actor string' would hand the capability to precisely the borrowed-actor attempt the gate exists to stop. Location is safe because the ledger is already readable by anyone holding the repository; the capability is not. | ATM-GOV-0365, whose delivery landed as 642d7382b. taskflow close --write refused twice: once with no lane (ATM_LANE_BORROWED_ACTOR_BLOCKED, no executing lane capability) and once under lane-20260813155237-claude-008-gov-0365-c080ef9223 (executing lane does not match the owner lane capability). Three lane sessions existed for the same actor. The authoritative one, lane-20260813154659-claude-008-gov-0365-cc1e754c97, was recorded in .atm/history/tasks/ATM-GOV-0365.json under claim.laneSession, together with its own exportHint. Reading the ledger resolved the block in one step; the refusal that had been paid twice never pointed there. Close then succeeded and sealed as 1f2674a50. | Fixed in source by ATM-GOV-0366. authorizeLaneCapability now attaches describeOwnerLaneSource to the refusal details: schema atm.laneCapabilityRefusalDisclosure.v1, carrying ownerClaimPath, ownerLaneRecorded, ownerLaneExportHintRecorded and a nextCommand that reads that record. The first draft of this item proposed disclosing the claim's exportHint to a caller whose --actor matched the owner actor; writing the test refuted it, because that caller is the borrowed-actor attempt by definition. The disclosure is therefore identical for every attempt and contains no lane value, which the regression asserts directly. Gate behaviour is unchanged; allowed stays false. The description is derived from the claim evaluateLaneCapability already read and is carried on the decision, so the refusal reports the source it consulted rather than reopening the ledger to describe itself — INV-ATM-012 at its smallest scale. Regression guard: test_atm_gov_0366_refusal_discloses_the_owner_lane_source in tests/cli/borrowed-actor-authority-hard-gate.test.ts, which also re-asserts that neither the owner lane key nor the owner lease appears in the thrown payload. Published in the sealed runner rebuilt by ATM-GOV-0367 as 652d48a5c, which also carried the source fixes then pending for [ATM-BUG-2026-08-13-013], [ATM-BUG-2026-08-13-015], [ATM-BUG-2026-08-13-019] and [ATM-BUG-2026-08-13-020]; those four remain their own owners to verify and close. Related to [ATM-BUG-2026-08-13-017], which covers the deeper defect that claim identity is not atomic across the ledger, runtime lock and direction lock; this item is the cheap disclosure half of it and can be fixed independently. Closing ATM-GOV-0366 produced fresh evidence for that item: tasks import --write --force preserved the claim, its lease id and its lane session — the ATM-BUG-2026-08-13-008 fix confirmed live in the frozen runner — yet the ledger came back with no workAdmissionTicket key, so the next governed commit failed ATM_WORK_ADMISSION_DELIVERY_NOT_AUTHORIZED. Two things are worth carrying into that item's fix. The refusal read as an identity mismatch although the ledger held no ticket at all, which points at the mismatch and missing branches not being distinguishable to the caller; and tasks renew is the governed reseal that recovers it, which is not named by the refusal. Neither observation was reduced to a mechanism here. A separate observation from the same close, not yet reduced to a cause and therefore not claimed here: the first close attempt under the correct lane reported ATM_TASKFLOW_CLOSE_WRITE_ROLLED_BACK after its backend reported ATM_TASKS_CLOSED, and an identical second invocation succeeded. If that recurs, capture both payloads before retrying — a write path whose second attempt succeeds is either correctly idempotent or silently order-dependent, and the two are not distinguishable from a single success. |
| ATM-BUG-2026-08-13-022 | 2026-08-13 | AI-Atomic-Framework | Bug | Medium | Open | Commit identity: the facade accepts an identity the hook does not, and its remediation names another actor | Two separate defects in one governed commit refusal. First, git commit --name/--email documents itself as seeding the actor runtime identity profile, but seeding it there does not satisfy the pre-commit attribution check: the same command that reported ATM_GIT_COMMIT_IDENTITY_MISSING accepted --name and --email on the retry, got past the facade, and was then blocked by ATM_COMMIT_IDENTITY_PROFILE_MISSING from the hook. Only a prior identity set --actor call resolved it. Second, the remediation template the hook prints substitutes a hard-coded example identity — git-name "Codex", git-email "codex@openai.local" — into a command that names the blocked actor. Copying the prescribed command verbatim registers the blocked actor under a different agent's name and email, and every commit it then authors is misattributed. | A facade option that advertises identity seeding must satisfy the same identity requirement the hook enforces, or must not advertise it. A generated remediation command must contain no identity value the caller did not supply: unknown fields belong in placeholders such as <git-name> and <git-email>, which fail loudly if pasted unedited, rather than in a real identity borrowed from whichever actor happened to seed the template. | ATM-GOV-0366 delivery commit, 2026-08-13. node atm.mjs git commit --actor claude-008-gov-0366 ... failed ATM_GIT_COMMIT_IDENTITY_MISSING. The retry adding --name "Claude 008" --email "claude-008@atm.local" failed ATM_GIT_COMMIT_FAILED, whose stderr carried the hook's blockingFindings entry ATM_COMMIT_IDENTITY_PROFILE_MISSING with requiredCommand: node atm.mjs identity set --actor "claude-008-gov-0366" --git-name "Codex" --git-email "codex@openai.local" --json. Running identity set with the actor's own name and email, then re-running the identical commit, produced 3baf721bf with the correct governed author. | Two independent fixes. Make the identity remediation template placeholder-only so no real identity can be inherited from the environment that produced it; a paste of the unedited command must fail rather than silently misattribute. Separately, decide whether git commit --name/--email seeds the profile the hook reads or stops advertising that it does, and cover the chosen answer with a focused test that runs the facade and the hook against the same unregistered actor. Adjacent to [ATM-BUG-2026-08-13-021] only in that both were found on the same delivery. |

| ATM-BUG-2026-08-13-025 | 2026-08-13 | AI-Atomic-Framework | Bug | High | Fixed in source; frozen publication pending | One command needing two emergency permissions cannot be authorized at all | tasks close with --allow-stale-runner and --historical-delivery selects two protected surfaces: the stale-runner path requires backend.runnerRecovery and the close itself requires backend.tasks.close. --emergency-approval binds exactly one lease, and each check reports only the permission it wanted, so every single-lease attempt is refused by whichever check the lease does not satisfy. Passing both leases does not help: the last one parsed wins and the other check fails. The emergency lane is therefore closed to any operation that legitimately needs two permissions, which is precisely the operation most likely to need it. | --emergency-approval must accept a set of leases and satisfy each protected surface from whichever lease authorizes it, or an approve call must be able to grant a bounded permission set in one lease. Each permission must stay individually bounded to task, actor and flags, and the refusal must name every permission still unsatisfied rather than only the first one checked — an operator who is told one permission at a time cannot tell a two-permission command from a wrong lease. | ATM-GOV-0367, 2026-08-13. Four leases were approved in sequence. EMG-ATM-GOV-0367-3ea985dbab (backend.tasks.close, --allow-stale-runner) refused with ATM_EMERGENCY_FLAG_NOT_APPROVED for --historical-delivery. EMG-ATM-GOV-0367-e3b96a26e7 (backend.tasks.close, both flags) refused with ATM_EMERGENCY_PERMISSION_MISMATCH: is for backend.tasks.close, not backend.runnerRecovery. EMG-ATM-GOV-0367-ba958cfa28 (backend.runnerRecovery, both flags) refused with the mirror image: is for backend.runnerRecovery, not backend.tasks.close. Passing ba958cfa28 and e3b96a26e7 together refused on whichever was named second. The card was delivered as 0cf861814 and could not be closed through this lane at all; the blocker was cleared by removing the stale-runner condition itself. Publication verified on 2026-08-14 against the runner's own declared input: release/atm-onefile/release-manifest.json at publication commit 8a3047111c7eb3673950ae719daed2801c151860 records sealedSourceCommit b3996c755027679acd995c4163fe96365f20468b with buildDecision fullRebuild and payloadReuse false, so the packed runner was rebuilt from that commit rather than reusing an earlier payload. The fix commit for this item, 50e806e85d8166cda1961d170bc4f6f16de3c8bd, is an ancestor of that sealed source commit. Corroborated inside the published tree by direct symbol: packages/cli/dist/commands/emergency/gate.js at 8a3047111c7eb3673950ae719daed2801c151860 contains selectLeaseForPermission and the suppliedLeases refusal payload. Independently confirmed in use: ATM-GOV-0368 was itself closed through the frozen runner by passing two lease ids as one comma-separated --emergency-approval value, which is the exact capability this item was refused. | Fixed in source by ATM-GOV-0368. The approval argument gained set semantics without changing its type: a single --emergency-approval value may carry comma-separated lease ids, and assertEmergencyApproval selects the member whose permission matches the surface being authorized, consuming only that member. Selection is the entire change — each selected lease is still validated for status, permission, task, actor, expiry, remaining uses and allowed flags exactly as a lone lease always was, so nothing an operator must approve became optional, and every call site that forwards the value as a string was left untouched. When no member matches, the refusal now lists every supplied lease with the permission it carries alongside the permission required, which is the same disclosure correction made for lane refusals in [ATM-BUG-2026-08-13-021]. Regression guard: test_atm_gov_0368_one_command_satisfies_every_permission_it_needs in packages/cli/src/commands/emergency/__tests__/gate.test.ts, which asserts the two-surface case selects a different lease per surface, that each is consumed exactly once, and that a set covering neither surface is still refused with no lease consumed. Before that fix existed, the only route was to remove the second condition rather than authorize it — republish the frozen runner so --allow-stale-runner is not needed — which is available only to an operator who can also take the release surface, and which was tried here and did not hold. ATM-GOV-0367 took the release surface, reserved the runner-sync queue head, rebuilt the sealed runner from f1fa1208c and committed the artifacts as 652d48a5c; the runner was current for roughly three minutes. A second lane then created an untracked file under scripts/, whose mtime post-dated the build, and the freshness check declared the runner stale again — the same live-WIP sensitivity recorded in [ATM-BUG-2026-08-13-019], here reached through the staleness check rather than through publication admission. The consequence is worth stating plainly: while any other lane is actively editing framework source, no amount of republishing makes a close reachable, and the emergency lane that exists for exactly that case is the dead end above. Either half alone is survivable; together they close every route. The mechanism is worth stating exactly, because it makes the deadlock permanent rather than transient: freshness is judged by mtime, while the sealed build is content-cached. Re-running the build at the new HEAD returned cacheHitSkip — correctly, since no framework source had changed — so no artifact mtime advanced and the runner stayed stale. Once any foreign file's mtime passes the last build, a lane that has changed no source cannot make the runner fresh again by rebuilding, because the only honest build is a no-op. The deadlock lifts only when someone else's genuine source change triggers a real rebuild. Related to [ATM-BUG-2026-08-13-013], the bootstrap circularity that makes --allow-stale-runner necessary in the first place, and to [ATM-BUG-2026-08-13-021] in that both are refusals that disclose one side of a two-sided comparison. |

| ATM-BUG-2026-08-15-003 | 2026-08-15 | AI-Atomic-Framework | Bug | P2 | Open | The atom map is exempt from the physical line budget that governs the source it accounts for | The governed commit path enforces a 600-line physical budget on touched source files and refuses with ATM_TOUCHED_PHYSICAL_LINE_BUDGET_BLOCKED. That enforcement observably does not reach the atomization ledger: an owner shard well past the threshold was amended and committed in the same operation whose source file was rejected for crossing it. The asymmetry matters because the atom map is not incidental data. It is the record of how the codebase is atomized, it is read to decide extraction boundaries, and it grows monotonically as coverage grows. A ledger that records atomization while being exempt from the discipline it records will eventually become the largest unatomized artifact in the repository, and the day the budget is extended to cover it, every map update becomes unfixable through the governed path — the update needed to shard it would itself be blocked. | State it as a rule rather than a threshold: any artifact that serves as the ledger of atomization must be subject to the same physical budget as the source it accounts for, or must carry a validated sharding strategy that keeps each shard within that budget and is enforced by a validator rather than by convention. Budget coverage must be decided by the role an artifact plays, not by its file extension. If generated projections are deliberately exempt because they are derived, that exemption must be explicit and must not extend to the hand-authored shards those projections are built from. | 2026-08-15, ATM-GOV-0394. Adding 56 lines to packages/cli/src/commands/git-governance/implementation/commit-command.ts took it from 595 to 651 and the governed commit refused with ATM_TOUCHED_PHYSICAL_LINE_BUDGET_BLOCKED (nestedFailure.errorSummary: 'Governed commit blocked because touched source files exceed the physical line budget (600).'). That refusal was correct and produced a genuine atomization: the file was extracted into a router plus two named lifecycle branches plus a purity contract, at 290/231/173/65 lines. In the same delivery, atomic_workbench/atomization-coverage/path-to-atom-map-shards/owner-shard-cli.json was amended from 1168 to 1180 lines and committed without objection. Both files were touched by one governed commit; only the source file was measured. | Decide the intended coverage rule first, then enforce it: either extend the touched-file budget to atomization ledger shards, or require and validate a sharding strategy for them. Sequencing matters here and should be recorded with the fix — an already-oversized shard must be split before the budget is extended to cover it, or the split itself becomes unreachable through the governed path, which is the same bootstrap circularity recorded in [ATM-BUG-2026-08-13-013]. Do not write the remedy against a specific shard or a specific line count; the subject is the class of artifacts that account for atomization. |

When the resolved directory is absent, the failure is reported as an unregistered series rather than as an unresolvable root, and the suggested recovery is to register a series that is in fact already registered under owner approval in the real root. Two consequences follow. An operator acting on the suggestion would create a duplicate series and a second planning authority for ids that already exist, which is exactly what the no-new-series rule forbids; and the true defect, a root that does not resolve, is never named, so the same misdiagnosis repeats for every command that touches planning. | Root resolution must fail closed on its own terms. A configured or inherited planning root that does not exist, or that exists without a series registry, must produce a distinct error naming the path it tried, why it was rejected, and which candidate roots were considered. Registry-derived facts already present in the repository outrank an unverified default: task cards carry a planning creation seal recording the root they were authored under, and that recorded root should be preferred, or at minimum offered, before any suggestion to create new planning structure. No recovery hint may propose registering a series without first checking whether that series is already registered in a reachable root. | 2026-08-16, creating the card that became ATM-GOV-0397. `node atm.mjs plan card create --series GOV --title <...> --dry-run --json` returned ATM_PLAN_SERIES_NOT_REGISTERED with series GOV, registryPath C:\Users\User\3klife-npc-brain\docs\ai_atomic_framework\series-registry.json, and suggestedCommand `node atm.mjs plan series register --prefix TASK-GOV --family-dir <family-dir> --plan <plan.md> --owner-approved --write --json`. | ATM-BUG-2026-08-16-002 | 2026-08-16 | AI-Atomic-Framework | Bug | P2 | Open | Planning source seal refreshes its digest and timestamp on amendment but never advances amendmentEpoch | A governed re-import of an amended task card updates the seal's contentDigest and sealedAt while leaving amendmentEpoch at its initial value. The field is therefore inert: it cannot distinguish a first sealing from an amended one, cannot order two amendments, and cannot tell a consumer how many times a contract has been revised. A digest proves only difference. It answers whether the bytes changed, not whether the change was a governed amendment, nor which of two observed seals is later. Any consumer that needs generation ordering — drift detection comparing a cached seal against the current one, a reviewer deciding whether acceptance criteria moved since a claim was taken, a downstream repository mirroring planning state — has no monotonic signal to read, and the one field designed to carry it silently reports zero. | The epoch must advance exactly when the sealed contract changes, and the seal write must be the only place that decides it, so no caller can produce a seal whose digest moved while its epoch stood still. A seal whose digest differs from its recorded predecessor while its epoch is unchanged is an internally inconsistent record and should be rejected rather than written. Whichever surface performs the amendment — a forced re-import, an explicit reseal, or a future amendment command — must go through that single writer. | 2026-08-16, ATM-GOV-0369 amendment 1. | ATM-BUG-2026-08-16-003 | 2026-08-16 | AI-Atomic-Framework | Bug | P2 | Open | A line-budget guard test asserts a threshold its own subject already exceeds at HEAD, so it is red on the mainline | A regression test pins a named module at or below the 600-line budget, and that module is already over the threshold in committed history. The assertion therefore fails on a clean checkout, independently of any working change. A guard that is red before anyone touches the repository carries no signal: it cannot distinguish a regression from the baseline it inherited, every contributor who runs it must first establish that the failure is not theirs, and the natural response after doing that twice is to stop running it. The budget rule itself is sound and was observed doing real work in the same session; the defect is that its enforcement was allowed to enter a permanently failing state rather than failing at the moment the subject crossed the line. This is distinct from the already-recorded claim-admission circularity: there the gate blocks the repair, here the gate has silently stopped guarding. | A budget assertion and its subject must not be able to diverge in committed history. Whichever check runs on the mainline should refuse the commit that first pushes a pinned module past its threshold, so the guard can never inherit a failing baseline. Where a baseline genuinely cannot be repaired immediately, the exemption must be explicit, recorded with its own owner and expiry, and visible in the test's own output — an acknowledged and bounded exception, not a red assertion that everyone learns to ignore. State it as the general rule: a guard is only evidence while it is green on an untouched checkout. | 2026-08-16, during ATM-GOV-0369. `node --strip-types tests/cli/pre-commit-hook-extraction.test.ts` failed with 'packages/cli/src/commands/hook/pre-commit/support.ts should stay at or below 600 lines, saw 606'. That file was not modified by the work in progress: `git status --porcelain -- packages/cli/src/commands/hook/pre-commit/support.ts` returned empty, so the worktree copy is byte identical to HEAD, and the assertion therefore fails identically on a clean checkout. The same session independently observed two further pre-existing red tests, git-commit-failure-residue-rollback and git-commit-nested-failure-recovery, proven not attributable to in-flight work by running them in an isolated worktree at clean HEAD. | Decide the intended enforcement point first — the commit-time budget gate, the test, or both — then make the mainline unable to hold a subject that violates its own pinned threshold. Do not repair this by raising the number in the assertion to match the file; that converts a guard into a record of whatever happened. Related to [ATM-BUG-2026-07-29-248] and [ATM-BUG-2026-07-18-003], which cover the opposite failure of the same budget: there admission refuses the very claim that would split an oversized file. Together they bound the rule from both ends, and a fix for either should state which end it addresses. |
| ATM-BUG-2026-08-16-004 | 2026-08-16 | AI-Atomic-Framework | Bug | High | Fixed — explicit new-claim scope now takes precedence over predecessor direction-lock scope; source and frozen runner verified | A fresh claim inherits the predecessor's work-admission ticket grant, which then silently narrows commit authority below the claim it is bound to | Commit authority is the intersection of the active claim's file list and the work-admission ticket's file-write grant: a non-empty grant filters the claim rather than describing it. When a task changes hands, the newly minted claim can carry a ticket whose grant still holds the predecessor's file set while its claimGeneration correctly matches the new lease. The ticket therefore looks internally consistent under every identity check — right task, right actor, right lease — yet authorizes a different body of work than the claim it belongs to. The consequence is silent and misleading rather than fail-closed: paths that sit squarely inside the live claim are reported to the operator as external dirty files, in the same list as unrelated repository noise. Nothing in the output names the ticket, the grant, or the intersection, so the observed symptom points at scope while the cause lives one layer away. An operator debugging this will re-check scope, re-read the direction lock, and find them correct, because they are. | A ticket's grant must be derived from the claim it is bound to, at the moment that claim is created, so a grant can never describe a different generation of work than its claimGeneration asserts. Where the two can still diverge, the divergence must be detected rather than applied: a grant narrower than its own active claim is an internally inconsistent authority record and should fail closed with a diagnostic that names both sets, not silently reclassify in-claim paths as foreign. State it as the general rule — when two authority records intersect to produce a decision, a refusal must identify which record excluded the path, because the operator cannot otherwise tell which one to repair. | 2026-08-16, ATM-GOV-0369 after an official handoff from cursor-captain to claude-008. Isolating the cause required reading commit-authority-scopes.ts, where a non-empty ticketScopes acts as an intersecting filter over an already-matched claim scope. Running the official `tasks scope add` with the same four paths returned ATM_SCOPE_AMENDMENT_APPLIED with addedPaths [] — every path was already present — while the ticket's file-write grant went from 11 entries to 28. No scope changed; only the stale ticket was re-minted, which isolates the ticket as the sole blocking factor. The bundle then resolved correctly. | Repaired by ce731c8f4: resolveTaskWorkAdmissionFiles now gives a fresh claim's explicit admitted files precedence over a prior direction-lock snapshot before minting the ticket grant. 2026-09-02 scope-amendment-work-admission-ticket.test.ts and work-admission-ticket-scope-glob-parity.test.ts passed; the same implementation is present in packages/cli/dist. Retain the ticket/claim intersection and continue improving refusal disclosure under [ATM-BUG-2026-08-13-003]; do not weaken ticket scope to treat an empty grant as permissive. |
| ATM-BUG-2026-08-16-005 | 2026-08-16 | AI-Atomic-Framework | Bug | P1 | Closed — post-HEAD-advance reconciliation re-verified | After a successful task-scoped commit, the index is restored to a snapshot taken before HEAD moved, leaving the just-committed files staged for deletion while the attempt reports no residue | The task-scoped commit transaction parks foreign staged entries, commits, then restores the index from a snapshot captured before the operation began. Restoration is correct for entries that existed in that snapshot, but it is applied against a HEAD that the commit itself has advanced. A file that was untracked before the commit has no entry in the snapshot, so restoring it means removing its index entry — and once that file is in the new HEAD, an absent index entry is no longer 'untracked', it is a staged deletion of content that was just committed. The parking mechanism protects foreign work exactly as designed; the fault is that the restore target was never rebased onto the new HEAD. What makes this expensive rather than merely wrong is the reporting: the attempt record reports committed with an empty residue rollback list, so the operation asserts a clean postcondition while the index disagrees with HEAD on every path it just wrote. An operator who trusts the receipt and commits again will delete the files they just added. | A restore that runs after HEAD has moved must reconcile against the post-operation HEAD, not replay a pre-operation snapshot verbatim. Paths the operation itself committed should end at parity with the new HEAD; paths it parked should return exactly as they were. The general rule is that a snapshot is only a valid restore target while the baseline it was taken against still holds — once the operation changes that baseline, restoration must be expressed relative to the new one. Independently, the postcondition must be verified rather than assumed: an attempt may only report an empty residue set after comparing the index against HEAD for the paths it touched, so that a divergence surfaces as a receipt instead of as a silent trap for the next commit. | 2026-08-16, ATM-GOV-0369, governed commit c30eb0820dbd560cf6959066b5e19a4619a84434 via `git commit --auto-stage`. The commit itself was correct: 11 files, exactly the resolved bundle, no foreign content. Immediately afterwards `git status --porcelain` reported the three newly added backlog item shards simultaneously as D (staged deletion) and ?? (untracked) — consistent with `git ls-files -s` returning no entry for them while `git cat-file -e HEAD:<path>` confirmed the content was in HEAD and the worktree copies were byte-identical to it. Three of the task's own task-event files landed in the same state, and the backlog projection showed MM. `git commit-status --json` reported status committed, phase committed, headAdvancedDuringAttempt true, errorCode null, and liveIndexResidueRollback []. The pre-existing staged deletion belonging to another task was preserved untouched throughout, before and after, confirming the parking half of the transaction works. Repair required re-adding the seven affected paths; the index then matched HEAD with the foreign staged deletion still the only remaining one. | Closed after re-verification on 2026-09-02: live-index reconciliation, index-lock contention, live-index drain, governed commit timeout/status, and signing-failure residue rollback all pass. The repair remains a8bbf3ad0aea8e738220350518029b03e4092e09 with recovery follow-up 2f8322cadcc893ae017b7bbd222361e399608736. A post-HEAD-advance commit no longer replays the pre-attempt live-index snapshot; historical residue is classified against the named commit parent and reconciled only when index/worktree bytes prove the safe transition. |
